When we tried DMing people with common scripts, we got 10 leads per 100 messages.

physique. That's your main goal. Would losing weight be considered a good goal or a bad goal?
The honest answer is it depends. It depends on the season you're in. If you're in a cut
season well it's a good goal. But if you're in a bulking season then losing weight is a bad goal.
It's important to have a main goal, a north star, something you aim towards. But to also understand
that it's not wise to get there linearly. If you do you'll end up with less than desirable results.
So if you're always bulking you get there too fat by the end of the year. If you're always cutting
then you build no muscle, right? It's more useful to look at business as the way business actually
is which is a non-linear phenomenon and understanding that to get to your main goal
you will need to cycle between seasons. The right answer depends on the season you're in
and you could have a situation in which one answer could be both correct and incorrect depending on
what timing you're you know watching the answer in. So as entrepreneurs I find that there's three
seasons. Bulk maintaining cut. Bulk is when it's time to get climbs. It's you have the infrastructure
ready and the time is right. You've got to be humble and experiment a lot. Imperfect offers
with perfect cadence is the move. This is usually Black Friday, the beginning of the year. That's
when you need to just step on the gas and just hold on to the rocket. Then there's maintain.
Maintain is when it's time to maintain the business running while you focus on other
areas of your life because something else calls you. Either a spiritual, emotional, physical,
relational journey, right? Maybe an emergency happened. For some of us if we dedicated half
the energy we dedicate into business to something else we would make massive progress. That's what
the maintain season is for. You keep the business running while you do other things that need your
more more urgent attention. And the last season is cut. Cut is when it's time to pay attention
to the details you overlook when bulking and maintaining. It's when you fine-tune the parts
of the business and prepare for a season of bulking. This is when you prep and the better
you prep during this phase by tweaking the small things the better your other two seasons are going
to be. Me now I'm in the cut season. I realized that there were many things I got away with in
easy times that could still be optimized. And this changes a lot about how I look at the things
happening in my business. In a bulk season I consider growing monthly recurring revenue the
biggest one. In a cut season I reframe that. I consider a deeper relationship with clients
and small optimizations like saving a churn, like a paid in full, like small things I consider those
the biggest ones. Now in summer it's kind of like the season sometimes gets imposed on us. So most
of you now are kind of in a cut phase, a cut season. We've got to optimize little things to
prepare ourselves for a bulk season. Because if you optimize the way you reduce churn or the way
you get paid in fulls, by the time fall comes when it's just so much easier to get clients,
your machine is so much more tuned that you prepare yourself for a glorious bulk.

So it's always framed as a plus.

And some rules of thumb for general pricing, I find that group programs tend to be really
